		<item>
		<title>The patch window is collapsing: Why security needs a new control plane</title>
		<link>https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/the-patch-window-is-collapsing-why-security-needs-a-new-control-plane/</link>
					<comments>https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/the-patch-window-is-collapsing-why-security-needs-a-new-control-plane/#respond</comments>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Igor Sakhnov]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Tue, 25 Aug 2026 16:00:00 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[Networking]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[AI]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/?p=53462</guid>

					<description><![CDATA[<p>Organizations need protection that operates in the gap between discovery and remediation.</p>
<p>The post <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/the-patch-window-is-collapsing-why-security-needs-a-new-control-plane/">The patch window is collapsing: Why security needs a new control plane</a> appeared first on <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog">Microsoft Azure Blog</a>.</p>
]]></description>
										<content:encoded><![CDATA[
<p class="wp-block-paragraph">For decades, cybersecurity defenders have relied on a relatively straightforward model: a vulnerability is disclosed, security teams assess exposure, test available fixes, deploy patches into production, and ultimately close the risk before attackers can exploit it at scale.&nbsp;</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">That model increasingly reflects a world that no longer exists. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Today&#8217;s enterprises operate thousands of interconnected workloads across hybrid and multicloud environments. Mission-critical applications power revenue-generating services, customer experiences, and core business operations that cannot simply be taken offline whenever a security update becomes available. At the same time, vulnerabilities are becoming more visible, more widely distributed, and more rapidly weaponized than ever before. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The result is a growing gap between how quickly organizations can safely remediate vulnerabilities and how quickly adversaries can exploit them.&nbsp;It is time to rethink how the industry approaches security during the critical period between disclosure and remediation.&nbsp;</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="the-patch-window-has-collapsed">The patch window has collapsed&nbsp;</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Traditional vulnerability management was built on the assumption that defenders could move faster than attackers. In many cases, they could. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">When a vulnerability was disclosed, organizations had time to understand the issue, assess affected systems, test patches, coordinate change windows, and deploy fixes before widespread exploitation occurred. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Today that timeline is rapidly shrinking. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Modern attack campaigns operate at internet scale. Security research, public disclosures, proof-of-concept exploits, and threat intelligence circulate globally within hours. A vulnerability announced in the morning can become the focus of active scanning and exploitation efforts by the afternoon. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Meanwhile, the operational realities of enterprise environments have not changed.&nbsp;Organizations still must:&nbsp;</p>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li class="wp-block-list-item">Understand the vulnerability and its business impact.&nbsp;</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item">Identify affected systems across large estates.&nbsp;</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item">Evaluate dependencies and compatibility concerns.&nbsp;</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item">Validate fixes in test environments.&nbsp;</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item">Coordinate deployment schedules.&nbsp;</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item">Monitor for regressions and operational risk.&nbsp;</li>
</ul>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">These are not signs of inefficiency. They are necessary safeguards for business-critical environments.&nbsp;The challenge is that while defensive processes continue to require days or weeks, offensive timelines are increasingly measured in hours.&nbsp;</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">That creates one of the most dangerous periods in modern cybersecurity: the window between awareness and remediation. </p>



<figure data-wp-context="{&quot;imageId&quot;:&quot;6a9235a3603cd&quot;}" data-wp-interactive="core/image" data-wp-key="6a9235a3603cd" class="wp-block-image aligncenter size-large wp-lightbox-container"><img fetchpriority="high" decoding="async" width="1024" height="576" data-wp-class--hide="state.isContentHidden" data-wp-class--show="state.isContentVisible" data-wp-init="callbacks.setButtonStyles" data-wp-on--click="actions.showLightbox" data-wp-on--load="callbacks.setButtonStyles" data-wp-on-window--resize="callbacks.setButtonStyles" src="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/1121922_In-blog-3-1024x576.jpg" alt="The process in which attackers move and how long patching takes." class="wp-image-53498" srcset="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/1121922_In-blog-3-1024x576.jpg 1024w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/1121922_In-blog-3-300x169.jpg 300w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/1121922_In-blog-3-768x432.jpg 768w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/1121922_In-blog-3-1536x864.jpg 1536w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/1121922_In-blog-3-809x455.jpg 809w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/1121922_In-blog-3.jpg 1920w" sizes="(max-width: 1024px) 100vw, 1024px" /><button

			</svg>
		</button></figure>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="ai-is-expanding-the-defender-s-challenge">AI is expanding the defender&#8217;s challenge&nbsp;</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">AI is helping organizations modernize operations, accelerate development, and improve security outcomes. But the same technological advances are also changing the economics of offensive operations.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Historically, transforming a newly disclosed vulnerability into an effective attack often required extensive manual research and deep technical expertise. Security researchers and attackers alike needed to analyze documentation, understand exploit conditions, study affected software, and develop attack techniques. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Many of those steps can now be accelerated. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">AI-assisted workflows can help analyze vulnerability disclosures, identify likely attack paths, evaluate technical dependencies, and summarize complex technical information far more quickly than traditional manual processes. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">As these capabilities become more accessible, the timeline between disclosure and exploitation continues to compress.&nbsp;The result is a structural imbalance.&nbsp;</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Defenders remain responsible for protecting entire environments that may include thousands of servers, applications, databases, containers, and network assets. Attackers only need to identify a single viable path to exploitation.&nbsp;</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">This asymmetry is driving organizations to ask an increasingly important question: <em>What happens before the patch is deployed?</em> </p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="why-existing-security-approaches-fall-short">Why existing security approaches fall short&nbsp;</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The security industry has invested heavily in improving visibility. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Organizations today have access to more vulnerability data, threat intelligence, analytics, and detection capabilities than ever before. Security platforms can rapidly identify affected systems, prioritize remediation, and alert defenders to emerging threats. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">These capabilities are essential.&nbsp;But awareness alone does not reduce exposure.&nbsp;Many organizations find themselves in a position where they know exactly which systems are vulnerable but cannot immediately patch them.&nbsp;</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">For example, a business-critical application may require extensive validation before updates can be deployed. A manufacturing system may depend on software that cannot be taken offline during production hours. A regulated environment may require additional testing and approval processes before changes can be implemented. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">In these situations, the challenge is not identifying risk. The challenge is reducing risk while remediation is still underway. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Visibility, detection, and prioritization help organizations understand the problem. They do not necessarily provide a mechanism for containing that risk immediately. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">As attack timelines continue to compress, the industry needs a complementary approach focused on exposure reduction rather than simply exposure awareness. </p>


<figure data-wp-context="{&quot;imageId&quot;:&quot;6a9235a361cbf&quot;}" data-wp-interactive="core/image" data-wp-key="6a9235a361cbf" class="wp-block-image aligncenter size-large wp-lightbox-container"><img decoding="async" data-wp-class--hide="state.isContentHidden" data-wp-class--show="state.isContentVisible" data-wp-init="callbacks.setButtonStyles" data-wp-on--click="actions.showLightbox" data-wp-on--load="callbacks.setButtonStyles" data-wp-on-window--resize="callbacks.setButtonStyles" alt="3 D network representa" src="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/Virtual-Patching-Image-Approved-1024x626.webp" alt='3 D network representation. Text reads "Pre-emptive, adaptive, autonomous."' class="wp-image-53482 webp-format" srcset="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/Virtual-Patching-Image-Approved-1024x626.webp 1024w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/Virtual-Patching-Image-Approved-300x183.webp 300w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/Virtual-Patching-Image-Approved-768x469.webp 768w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/Virtual-Patching-Image-Approved-1536x939.webp 1536w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/Virtual-Patching-Image-Approved-2048x1252.webp 2048w" data-orig-alt="3 D network representa" src="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/Virtual-Patching-Image-Approved-1024x626.webp"><button
			class="lightbox-trigger"
			type="button"
			aria-haspopup="dialog"

			data-wp-init="callbacks.initTriggerButton"

			</svg>
		</button></figure>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="why-the-network-is-emerging-as-the-fastest-control-plane">Why the network is emerging as the fastest control plane&nbsp;</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">When a workload cannot immediately defend itself, another layer must help provide protection.&nbsp;Increasingly, organizations are looking to the network.&nbsp;</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Unlike endpoint-based controls, network-level protections operate around workloads rather than inside them. This distinction becomes particularly important during periods of elevated risk. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The network already understands communication patterns, connectivity requirements, trust relationships, and traffic flows. It sits at a strategic position where organizations can influence how systems interact with one another without necessarily modifying the applications themselves. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">This creates opportunities to reduce exploitability while remediation efforts are underway.&nbsp;Network-enforced protections can help:&nbsp;</p>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li class="wp-block-list-item">Restrict access to vulnerable systems.&nbsp;</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item">Limit exposure to potential attack paths.&nbsp;</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item">Reduce opportunities for lateral movement.&nbsp;</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item">Segment high-risk assets.&nbsp;</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item">Contain potential blast radius.&nbsp;</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item">Adjust controls dynamically as new information becomes available.&nbsp;</li>
</ul>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Perhaps most importantly, network controls can often be implemented significantly faster than enterprise software patches can be validated and deployed. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The objective is not to avoid patching. The objective is to create a meaningful layer of defense during the period when patching has not yet been completed.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">As AI compresses the time between vulnerability disclosure and exploitation, organizations need a defensive layer that can act immediately, without waiting for every workload to be patched, every application to be modified, or every endpoint agent to understand a new threat.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The network is uniquely positioned to become that control point: it already sits in the path of communication, has visibility across heterogeneous workloads, and can enforce protections consistently across large cloud estates without changing the applications themselves. More importantly, network controls can increasingly move beyond simple IP, port, and signature-based blocking toward context-aware, adaptive enforcement that constrains the specific behavior an exploit depends on while preserving legitimate traffic.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Consider an HTTP/2 denial-of-service vulnerability: the safest interim guidance may be to disable HTTP/2 entirely until systems are patched, but that can carry significant application and performance impact. A more precise network and workload-aware response could instead bound the exploitable behavior—limiting concurrent streams, tightening request constraints, or rate-limiting abusive connection patterns—while keeping the service available. This is why the network is becoming more than a connectivity layer: it can serve as a programmable, ubiquitous enforcement fabric that buys organizations the most valuable commodity during a zero-day—the time to patch safely.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">In an era where vulnerabilities may be weaponized within hours, every day of risk reduction matters. </p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="the-rise-of-adaptive-security">The rise of adaptive security&nbsp;</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The next evolution of cybersecurity is unlikely to rely solely on static policies or manual response processes.&nbsp;Modern environments are simply too large, dynamic, and interconnected.&nbsp;</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Organizations increasingly need security systems capable of understanding risk, evaluating context, and adapting protections as conditions change.&nbsp;This shift points toward a broader industry trend: adaptive security.&nbsp;</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Adaptive security systems aim to move beyond predefined rules toward continuously improving risk management. Rather than treating every vulnerability equally, they seek to understand the specific conditions that make a flaw exploitable and determine the most effective way to reduce exposure.&nbsp;At a high level, these systems must solve three critical challenges.&nbsp;</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph" id="first-they-must-understand-the-vulnerability-itself"><strong>First, they must understand the vulnerability itself.&nbsp;</strong></p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">This requires ingesting information from security advisories, vulnerability disclosures, threat intelligence, exploit research, and other sources to develop a meaningful understanding of how a threat operates. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph" id="second-they-must-correlate-that-understanding-with-real-world-environments"><strong>Second, they must correlate that understanding with real-world environments.&nbsp;</strong></p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">A vulnerability only becomes a material risk when specific systems, configurations, connectivity paths, and exposure conditions exist. Understanding this context is essential to determining actual risk. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph"><strong>Third, they must translate intelligence into action.&nbsp;</strong></p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Insight without enforcement provides limited value. The ultimate goal is to reduce exposure through controls that can be applied quickly, consistently, and at scale. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">AI is expected to play a significant role throughout this process, not merely as an analytical tool, but as an enabling technology that helps security systems understand complex relationships and make informed decisions faster than would otherwise be possible. </p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="looking-at-the-future-of-cybersecurity">Looking at the future of cybersecurity</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The cybersecurity industry has spent decades improving vulnerability management, patch deployment, and security operations. Those investments remain essential and will continue to be foundational elements of every organization&#8217;s security strategy. But the environment around us is changing. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Attackers are moving faster. Infrastructure is becoming more complex. AI is compressing timelines across the entire threat landscape.&nbsp;In this new reality, organizations cannot rely on patching alone.&nbsp;</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The future of cybersecurity will depend on an organization&#8217;s ability to reduce risk during the time between disclosure and remediation. Success will come from combining strong patch management practices with compensating controls capable of responding at machine speed. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The organizations that thrive will be those that treat security as a continuous, adaptive process rather than a sequence of point-in-time responses.&nbsp;The fundamental question is no longer whether vulnerabilities will emerge. They will.&nbsp;</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The question is how effectively organizations can protect themselves while they work to eliminate them.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">As the patch window continues to collapse, the industry will need new approaches that complement traditional remediation strategies, reduce exposure quickly, and help defenders regain the one resource that has become increasingly scarce in modern cybersecurity: time.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Microsoft is investing in new and innovative capabilities able to provide immediate protection from the storm, buying organizations the time they need to safely validate and deploy a permanent patch without exposing their environment to unnecessary risk.</p>
<p>The post <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/the-patch-window-is-collapsing-why-security-needs-a-new-control-plane/">The patch window is collapsing: Why security needs a new control plane</a> appeared first on <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog">Microsoft Azure Blog</a>.</p>

					<description><![CDATA[<p>Cloud-native platforms are becoming the foundation for AI transformation. Discover how Microsoft's Azure application platform helps organizations modernize, innovate, and operate AI-powered applications at scale.</p>
<p>The post <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/microsoft-named-a-leader-in-the-2026-gartner-magic-quadrant-for-cloud-native-application-platforms/">Microsoft named a Leader in the 2026 Gartner® Magic Quadrant™ for Cloud-Native Application Platforms </a> appeared first on <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog">Microsoft Azure Blog</a>.</p>
]]></description>
										<content:encoded><![CDATA[
<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Microsoft has been named a <a href="https://www.gartner.com/doc/reprints?id=00ThR000008aYRiUAM&amp;ct=260803&amp;st=sb">Leader in the 2026 Gartner® Magic Quadrant™ for Cloud-Native Application Platforms</a>, which we believe recognizes the platforms organizations rely on to build, deploy, and operate cloud-native applications at scale. This is our third consecutive year positioned as a Leader in this report.</p>



<div class="wp-block-buttons is-content-justification-center is-layout-flex wp-container-core-buttons-is-layout-a89b3969 wp-block-buttons-is-layout-flex">
<div class="wp-block-button"><a class="wp-block-button__link wp-element-button" href="https://www.gartner.com/reprints/?id=00ThR000008aYRiUAM&amp;ct=260803&amp;st=sb" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Read the report</a></div>
</div>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">We are proud of this recognition. More importantly, we believe it reflects a shift we see across industries. Cloud-native application platforms are no longer where organizations build and run modern applications. They are becoming the foundation for AI transformation.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The challenge is not a shortage of AI ideas. It is turning those ideas into production systems that can connect to existing applications and data, perform reliably at global scale, and meet the security and governance standards the business already expects. That requires more than a collection of application services. It requires a platform that brings application modernization, AI innovation, operations, and security together.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Microsoft&#8217;s cloud-native application platform is designed around that reality. <a href="https://go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?linkid=2288754">Azure App Service</a> provides a managed foundation for enterprise web applications and modernization. <a href="https://go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?linkid=2288742">Container Apps</a> runs cloud-native applications, APIs, AI inferencing, and agents without requiring teams to manage infrastructure. <a href="https://go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?linkid=2288702">Azure Functions</a> provides event-driven execution and integration, while <a href="https://go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?linkid=2288563">API Management</a> governs APIs, models, and agent tools through a consistent policy layer. Together with <a href="https://go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?linkid=2288830">Microsoft Foundry</a>, <a href="https://go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?linkid=2288905">GitHub Copilot</a>, and the shared Azure foundation for identity, networking, observability, and security, these capabilities give organizations one platform for what they already run and what they build next.</p>


<figure data-wp-context="{&quot;imageId&quot;:&quot;6a9235a369060&quot;}" data-wp-interactive="core/image" data-wp-key="6a9235a369060" class="wp-block-image aligncenter size-large wp-lightbox-container"><img decoding="async" data-wp-class--hide="state.isContentHidden" data-wp-class--show="state.isContentVisible" data-wp-init="callbacks.setButtonStyles" data-wp-on--click="actions.showLightbox" data-wp-on--load="callbacks.setButtonStyles" data-wp-on-window--resize="callbacks.setButtonStyles" src="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/MQ-Cloud-Native-App-Platform-Blog-Thumbnail-2-1024x1024.webp" alt="Gartner Magic Quadrant for Cloud-Native Application Platforms (July 2026) showing Microsoft positioned in the Leaders quadrant." class="wp-image-53404 webp-format" srcset="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/MQ-Cloud-Native-App-Platform-Blog-Thumbnail-2-1024x1024.webp 1024w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/MQ-Cloud-Native-App-Platform-Blog-Thumbnail-2-300x300.webp 300w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/MQ-Cloud-Native-App-Platform-Blog-Thumbnail-2-150x150.webp 150w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/MQ-Cloud-Native-App-Platform-Blog-Thumbnail-2-768x768.webp 768w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/MQ-Cloud-Native-App-Platform-Blog-Thumbnail-2.webp 1080w" data-orig-src="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/MQ-Cloud-Native-App-Platform-Blog-Thumbnail-2-1024x1024.webp"><button

<p class="has-text-align-center has-body-medium-font-size wp-block-paragraph">Gartner® Magic Quadrant™ graphic for Cloud-Native Applications Platform 2026</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="build-ai-apps-and-agents-faster">Build AI apps and agents faster</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Microsoft brings the application runtime and AI toolchain together so developers can build with the model, framework, and architecture that fits the job. <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/ai-foundry" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Foundry</a> provides the models, agent tooling, evaluation, tracing, and safety capabilities needed to take AI systems into production. GitHub Copilot helps developers move faster across the development lifecycle. Azure’s application platform provides the managed runtime, event-driven execution, integration, and API capabilities to turn those systems into applications people can rely on.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">This is where platform capabilities matter. Developers can deploy a container directly to a production endpoint with <a href="https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/blog/appsonazureblog/introducing-azure-container-apps-express/4519150" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure Container Apps Express</a>, and use Azure Functions to expose existing business logic through the Model Context Protocol. More than <a href="https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/blog/appsonazureblog/announcing-managed-connectors-for-azure-functions-preview/4523798" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">1,400 connectors help agents</a> act across enterprise systems without requiring developers to rebuild authentication, retries, and integration logic for every connection.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph"><a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/container-apps/sandboxes-overview" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure Container Apps Sandboxes</a> provide the isolated compute that agent platforms run on. The same primitive runs the agent itself, hosts its tools and MCP servers, and executes the code it generates, each in its own microVM, hardware-isolated boundary, with state that survives when the agent pauses. It is the compute layer behind <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/ai-foundry/agent-service" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Foundry Agent Service</a> and is available to customers building and operating their own agent platforms on Azure.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The result is a shorter path from an AI idea to a governed application or agent that can deliver on your business goals.</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="modernize-applications-regardless-of-architecture">Modernize applications regardless of architecture</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">AI transformation starts with the application estate organizations already have, not a blank slate. Azure gives teams a practical path to modernize at their own pace. They can move established applications to fully managed Platform as a Service (PaaS) services, containerize where it makes sense, adopt event-driven patterns incrementally, and extend existing business logic so it can participate in AI-powered experiences. <a href="https://aka.ms/MIonAppSvcGA" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">App Service Managed Instance</a> helps organizations move complex Windows and .NET applications without requiring a rewrite, while <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/solutions/application-and-database-modernization/#Products-3" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">GitHub Copilot app modernization</a> accelerates assessment and remediation. Once modernized, those applications can connect to the same data, AI services, APIs, identity controls, and operational practices as new cloud-native applications.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">This flexibility protects the value already in the application estate while creating a foundation for continuous innovation. Organizations do not have to choose between modernizing the core and building for the AI era. The platform makes those efforts part of the same strategy.</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="secure-govern-and-simplify-operations">Secure, govern, and simplify operations</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">AI applications raise the operational bar. Agents can call APIs, execute generated code, and interact with sensitive systems at a speed and volume that traditional controls were not designed to manage. Security, governance, and observability cannot be added after deployment. They need to be part of the platform.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Azure provides shared identity, networking, policy, and security controls across the application estate. <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/api-management" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure API Management</a> extends that consistency to APIs, MCP servers, and model endpoints. Its AI gateway capabilities help teams authenticate access, enforce token limits and quotas, balance traffic across models, apply semantic caching, and monitor how AI services are consumed. <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/container-apps/sandboxes-overview" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure Container Apps Sandboxes</a> add hardware-level isolation for agent-generated or untrusted code, while confidential computing and <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-ie/security/business/microsoft-defender" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Defender</a> strengthen protection for sensitive workloads.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Operations also need to keep pace with development. Azure combines global reach with managed scaling across web apps, containers, functions, and APIs. Built-in load balancing, zone redundancy, deployment controls, and integrated observability help teams maintain performance as usage grows. <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/monitor/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure Monitor and Application Insights</a> give teams end-to-end visibility across applications and AI workloads. <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/sre-agent" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure SRE Agent</a> brings agentic operations into that environment by helping teams investigate incidents, identify root causes, and take auditable remediation actions. Together with <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/github/copilot" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">GitHub Copilot</a>, these capabilities create a secure and reliable path across the application lifecycle, from development to production operations.</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="customer-momentum-across-the-platform">Customer momentum across the platform</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Across banking, retail, healthcare, manufacturing, IT, and other industries, organizations are running their most critical applications and their newest AI workloads on the same Azure foundation.</p>



<blockquote class="wp-block-quote has-quote-default-font-size is-layout-flow wp-block-quote-is-layout-flow">
<p class="wp-block-paragraph">A clean cloud architecture is a prerequisite for AI. You can&#8217;t build on top of a weak foundation. </p>
<cite>Mike Gibson, Chief Technology Officer, Planet DDS</cite></blockquote>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph"><a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en/customers/story/26987-planet-dds-azure" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Planet DDS</a> built that foundation on Azure. It modernized its platform on <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/app-service" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure App Service</a> and cut provisioning time from six weeks to one day, which gives its teams room to invest in the product rather than the plumbing. <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en/customers/story/26105-replit-azure-openai-in-foundry-models" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Replit</a> and <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Azure</a> now put enterprise software building in reach of every Hexaware employee, extending development beyond the people who write code for a living.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">That same platform is already running AI in production. <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en/customers/story/25676-commerzbank-ag-azure-ai-foundry-agent-service" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Commerzbank</a> runs its agentic AI architecture on <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/container-apps" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure Container Apps</a>, where its Ava assistant handles more than 30,000 customer conversations each month and resolves 75% of them autonomously. <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en/customers/story/26778-ghassan-aboud-holding-microsoft-foundry" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Ghassan Aboud Group</a> built its Ragin AI platform on Azure Container Apps to deliver agentic customer experiences across its businesses. <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en/customers/story/26647-levi-strauss-and-co-microsoft-foundry" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Levi Strauss and Co.</a> uses Microsoft Foundry to simplify everyday work and accelerate decision-making. These are not experiments running alongside the business. They are AI systems operating inside it.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Taken together, these stories describe one platform doing several jobs at once. It carries existing applications forward, runs new AI and agentic workloads in production, and gives organizations a common foundation to operate both with confidence.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">We believe the next generation of applications will be cloud-native, AI-powered, and deeply connected to the systems organizations already rely on. In our view, Microsoft&#8217;s position as a <a href="https://www.gartner.com/doc/reprints?id=00ThR000008aYRiUAM&amp;ct=260803&amp;st=sb">Leader for the third consecutive year</a> reinforces our commitment to giving every organization one secure, scalable platform to modernize what it has and build what comes next.</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="get-started">Get started</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Wherever you are in your journey, there is a place to begin today.</p>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Building AI apps and agents</strong>.&nbsp;Start on <a href="https://go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?linkid=2288742" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure Container Apps</a> to build your agentic applications with built-in enterprise security and ultra-fast scale.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Modernizing existing applications</strong>.&nbsp;Bring Windows and .NET workloads forward with <a href="https://aka.ms/MIonAppSvcGA">App Service Managed Instance</a>, and use GitHub Copilot app modernization to accelerate assessment and remediation.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Running event-driven and API-centric workloads</strong>.&nbsp;Build with <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/azure-functions/functions-create-ai-enabled-apps?pivots=programming-language-csharp">Azure Functions</a> and manage your APIs, MCP servers, and model endpoints through Azure API Management.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Operating with confidence</strong>.&nbsp;Add <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/sre-agent/create-and-set-up" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure SRE Agent</a> and <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/monitor" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure Monitor</a> to bring agentic operations and end-to-end observability to what you already run.</li>
</ul>

					<description><![CDATA[<p>Learn how AI cost management helps organizations move from AI pilots to measurable ROI through greater visibility, governance, and optimization.</p>
<p>The post <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/the-economics-of-agent-optimization-from-pilots-to-measurable-returns/">The Economics of Agent Optimization: From pilots to measurable returns</a> appeared first on <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog">Microsoft Azure Blog</a>.</p>
]]></description>
										<content:encoded><![CDATA[
<p class="wp-block-paragraph"><em>This blog post is the first of a four-part series called <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/tag/the-economics-of-agent-optimization/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">The Economics of Agent Optimization</a> which shares the strategies, capabilities, and proof points to help you optimize agent costs and run AI as a managed investment system on Microsoft Foundry.</em></p>



<hr class="wp-block-separator has-alpha-channel-opacity" />



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The AI conversation in most enterprises has moved from the whiteboard to the budget review. Two years ago, the question was whether AI could work. The question leaders are asking now is sharper and less comfortable: is it paying for itself? </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">For the teams now in production—including more than 100,000 organizations <a href="https://ai.azure.com/home" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">building on Microsoft Foundry</a> that question has become urgent. Tokens have become the new unit of technology spend, and financial discipline (not model choice) is what decides whether a promising pilot ever scales. The money is already moving in: in a <a href="https://blogs.microsoft.com/blog/2025/11/11/bridging-the-ai-divide-how-frontier-firms-are-transforming-business/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Microsoft-commissioned IDC study</a> of more than 4,000 business leaders, 71% said they plan to increase AI budgets, funded from IT and non-IT sources alike. The budgets are growing. The question is whether the discipline grows with them. </p>



<figure class="wp-block-pullquote has-electric-blue-300-color has-text-color has-link-color wp-elements-2614fd590e882918aba662d7868b0f7c" style="border-top-left-radius:0px;border-top-right-radius:0px;border-bottom-left-radius:0px;border-bottom-right-radius:0px;padding-top:var(--wp--preset--spacing--md);padding-bottom:var(--wp--preset--spacing--md)"><blockquote><p><strong><em>71% of business leaders plan to increase their AI budgets</em></strong></p><cite>2025 IDC survey</cite></blockquote></figure>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  The teams pulling ahead did not go looking for a cheaper model. They stopped running AI as a string of one-off pilots and started running it as a managed investment system: every request sized to its job, every agent improved as it runs, and every dollar bounded and accounted for. That shift, from buying intelligence to managing it, is the whole game. This series is about how the system works and why Microsoft Foundry is built to run it.
</p>



<div class="wp-block-buttons is-content-justification-center is-layout-flex wp-container-core-buttons-is-layout-a89b3969 wp-block-buttons-is-layout-flex">
<div class="wp-block-button"><a class="wp-block-button__link wp-element-button" href="https://ai.azure.com/home" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Start building on Microsoft Foundry</a></div>
</div>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="understand-your-ai-costs-and-spending">Understand your AI costs and spending</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  Before you can manage AI spend, you need to understand what creates it. Cost is not determined only by the model you choose. It is also shaped by the application or agent built around that model.
</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  Every request includes input tokens, such as system prompts, conversation history, tool definitions, and retrieved content, as well as output tokens generated by the model. Because models are stateless, the full context is sent with every request. Costs can increase over time even when the user asks only a simple follow-up question.
</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  Agents introduce another layer of complexity. Instead of following a single path, an agent may evaluate options, retry actions, or call multiple tools before producing a response. A single user request can generate many model calls, making workflow design as important as model selection.
</p>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="improve-ai-cost-visibility-across-teams">Improve AI cost visibility across teams</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  AI spend is difficult to manage when it appears as a single aggregate number. Teams need visibility into costs by application, agent, workflow, and model to understand what is driving usage and where optimization opportunities exist.
</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  Without that level of attribution, it becomes difficult to explain costs, prioritize improvements, or measure the impact of optimization efforts.
</p>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="control-and-optimize-spend">Control and optimize spend</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  Visibility alone is not enough. AI workloads can scale quickly, and unexpected behavior can increase consumption in a short period of time. Organizations need controls that help manage spend before costs become a surprise.
</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  Optimization also requires more than selecting a lower-cost model. Most AI workloads contain a mix of requests with different requirements. Better outcomes come from matching requests to the right models, reducing unnecessary context, limiting unneeded tool use, and improving agent workflows so they operate more efficiently.
</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="why-microsoft-is-the-platform-for-ai-finops">Why Microsoft is the platform for AI FinOps</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">FinOps began as the discipline of bringing financial accountability to variable cloud spend, a shared operating model that puts engineering, finance, and product on one set of numbers. FinOps for AI comes down to four commitments:</p>



<ol class="wp-block-list">
<li class="wp-block-list-item">Make AI predictable to fund</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item">Efficient by design</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item">Optimized at scale</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item">Proven in value </li>
</ol>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Microsoft&#8217;s answer is a single, first-party approach to FinOps for AI that spans the entire lifecycle—plan, build, manage, and measure. Cost visibility and control are built into the products teams already use: Microsoft Foundry and GitHub where agents are built and run, <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/cost-management/?ef_id=_k_Cj0KCQjw-MDTBhCgARIsAKAkdlTvgPpAWE0VF6esP6uE_g62gdg7V-YSoF9em5d7AwVduHjni5qiVQ4aAoFOEALw_wcB_k_&amp;OCID=AIDcmm2gz5ejpc_SEM__k_Cj0KCQjw-MDTBhCgARIsAKAkdlTvgPpAWE0VF6esP6uE_g62gdg7V-YSoF9em5d7AwVduHjni5qiVQ4aAoFOEALw_wcB_k_&amp;gad_source=1&amp;gad_campaignid=21496728177&amp;gbraid=0AAAAADcJh_sTdbGVz4yQLKUFm2AuSzSJ6&amp;gclid=Cj0KCQjw-MDTBhCgARIsAKAkdlTvgPpAWE0VF6esP6uE_g62gdg7V-YSoF9em5d7AwVduHjni5qiVQ4aAoFOEALw_wcB" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Cost Management</a> for allocation and chargeback, <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/pricing/?ef_id=_k_Cj0KCQjw-MDTBhCgARIsAKAkdlRNGEoqyHl1k5hCpZA5Zn48ALL5otVcT5jfO5HLFk5a-a4XtWbf1mMaAo9qEALw_wcB_k_&amp;OCID=AIDcmm2gz5ejpc_SEM__k_Cj0KCQjw-MDTBhCgARIsAKAkdlRNGEoqyHl1k5hCpZA5Zn48ALL5otVcT5jfO5HLFk5a-a4XtWbf1mMaAo9qEALw_wcB_k_&amp;gad_source=1&amp;gad_campaignid=21496728177&amp;gbraid=0AAAAADcJh_sTdbGVz4yQLKUFm2AuSzSJ6&amp;gclid=Cj0KCQjw-MDTBhCgARIsAKAkdlRNGEoqyHl1k5hCpZA5Zn48ALL5otVcT5jfO5HLFk5a-a4XtWbf1mMaAo9qEALw_wcB" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure pricing</a> offers for commitment-based savings, and <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/api-management" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure API Management</a> as the gateway that meters and governs AI traffic. <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-agent-365" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Agent 365</a> extends the same discipline to the tenant—unifying agent cost management across Microsoft and third-party platforms with spending policies, budget caps, and departmental chargeback in one place. Together they give organizations something no point tool can: comprehensive, best-in-class cost management across the whole AI estate, from the first prompt to the board-level ROI number.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Foundry is where that approach gets specific, because it&#8217;s where agents are run and optimized. It runs AI as a managed investment system across one closed loop: <strong>optimize each request at runtime, optimize each agent workflow over time, and govern the spend continuously</strong>.</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="ai-cost-optimization-starts-with-visibility-1">AI cost optimization starts with visibility</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">A managed investment system makes three decisions, each at a different speed. You optimize the request in the moment it runs. You optimize the agent workflow over days and weeks, as you learn what works. And you govern the spend continuously, with limits and budgets that never sleep. Foundry is built to make all three. Each move has its own set of Foundry capabilities, and the map below shows how they fit together. </p>



<figure class="wp-block-table is-style-stripes"><table class="has-fixed-layout"><thead><tr><th><strong>The decision</strong></th><th><strong>What Foundry gives you</strong><span style="font-size: 14px"></span></th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td><strong>Optimize the request, at runtime</strong><br><em>Right-size every call so simple work never pays frontier prices.</em><br></td><td><ul><li><strong>Model router for Microsoft Foundry</strong> routes each prompt across cost, quality, and balanced modes, so simple requests don&#8217;t pay frontier-model prices.</li></ul><br><ul><li><strong>Deployment and pricing options</strong> match each workload to its cost and latency needs, spanning Global, Data Zone, and Regional deployments and the Standard, Priority, Provisioned Throughput, and Batch processing modes.</li></ul><br><ul><li><strong>Prompt and semantic caching</strong> reuse repeated context instead of paying to recompute it.</li></ul><br><ul><li><strong>Fine-tuning</strong> lets a smaller tuned model match a larger one on your task, lowering the per-token rate and shortening prompts.</li></ul><br><ul><li><strong>Microsoft IQ </strong>provides a shared enterprise intelligence layer across how people work, how the business operates, institutional knowledge, and the web. Within that layer, Foundry IQ gives agents reusable, permission-aware knowledge bases and uses agentic retrieval to select only the most relevant context. This improves grounding while reducing unnecessary input tokens.</li></ul></td></tr><tr><td><strong>Optimize the workflow, over time</strong><br><em>Make each agent cheaper as it learns what works.</em> <br></td><td><ul><li><strong>Agent optimizer</strong> tests prompts, models, tools, and skills against your own evaluators and promotes the best configuration, often holding quality on a smaller, cheaper model. </li></ul><br><ul><li><strong>Toolboxes</strong> send only the tools a request needs instead of every definition. </li></ul><br><ul><li><strong>Memory </strong>(procedural, user, and session memory) carries context across turns without resending the full history. </li></ul></td></tr><tr><td><strong>Govern the spend, continuously </strong><br><em>Set limits and budgets that hold, so no agent can run away with the bill.</em> <br></td><td><ul><li><strong>Azure API Management&#8217;s AI Gateway</strong> can be deployed in front of your Foundry endpoints as a separate AI Gateway layer, applying token rate limits, quotas, and caching for teams that already standardize on Azure API Management. We are working to deliver more seamless and integrated AI Gateway functionalities in Foundry.</li></ul><br><ul><li><strong>Foundry in-platform budgets and enforcement</strong> will be available soon to bring spending limits and enforcement natively into Foundry, closer to where agents run. </li></ul><br><ul><li>Foundry gives you model- and deployment-level cost reporting today, with Azure Cost Management as the system of record for budgets, alerts, and billed costs. Richer attribution, down to the individual agent and session, is on the roadmap. </li></ul></td></tr></tbody></table></figure>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Agent 365 will extend governance to the tenant, unifying cost management across Microsoft and third-party agents with spending policies, budget caps, and departmental chargeback.  </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  You can watch the runtime levers work live in our <a href="https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=mB0IyELzjRg">new Microsoft Mechanics episode on token economics</a>. 
</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="the-four-questions-ai-leaders-should-be-asking">The four questions AI leaders should be asking</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  If you take one thing from this post, take these four questions into your next AI or budget review. Each has a concrete answer in Foundry. If you cannot answer one today, that is where to start.
</p>



<ol class="wp-block-list">
<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Do we know what we&#8217;re paying for?</strong><br>Spend should be visible by model, agent, and workflow, not hidden in a single invoice line. Foundry&#8217;s metering and traces make it easier to understand where costs originate.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Are we paying the right amount for each request?</strong><br>Most requests do not need a frontier model. Model router, deployment and pricing options, caching, fine-tuning, and Foundry IQ help match each request to the capability it needs.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Are our agents operating efficiently?</strong><br>Agent costs should improve over time as workflows become more effective. Agent optimizer and memory in <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/ai-foundry/agent-service/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Foundry Agent Service</a> and <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/agents/how-to/tools/toolbox?pivots=python" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Toolboxes in Foundry</a> help reduce unnecessary token usage and improve execution quality.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Do our limits hold when usage spikes?</strong><br>Usage that expands rapidly needs controls that hold. Today, many teams put Azure API Management in front of their AI endpoints to enforce token rate limits and quotas at the AI Gateway layer. Native budgets and enforcement inside Foundry, plus tenant-wide controls through Agent 365, are where we are headed next.</li>
</ol>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  The first question is about understanding AI spend. The next three are the areas this series explores in more detail: matching requests to the right models, improving agent efficiency, and applying governance controls to manage cost at scale.
</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="get-started">Get started</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  This series will continue over the coming weeks, going one level deeper on each subsequent move: how to optimize the request at runtime, how to build agents that use tokens efficiently, and how to govern the spend as you scale. Each post pairs the thinking with the Foundry capabilities that make it real.
</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">You don&#8217;t have to wait to start. The capabilities behind this framework are live in <a href="https://ai.azure.com/home" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Foundry</a> today: </p>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li class="wp-block-list-item">Learn more about the ways to <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/control-plane/how-to-optimize-cost-performance" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">optimize model cost and performance</a> in Microsoft Foundry.  </li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item">Watch the <a href="https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=mB0IyELzjRg">token economics episode on <strong>Microsoft Mechanics</strong></a> for a hands-on look at the levers in action. </li>
</ul>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Follow along as the series unfolds and bring the four questions to your next review.</p>

<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Microsoft has been named a <a href="https://www.gartner.com/reprints/?id=00ThR000008k1rGUAQ&amp;ct=260804&amp;st=sb" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Leader in the inaugural 2026 Gartner® Magic Quadrant™ for AI-Augmented Code Modernization Tools</a>, which recognizes software solutions that use specialized AI agents, generative AI, and deterministic analysis to accelerate the transformation of legacy systems. We are proud of our placement as a Leader in this first edition of the report.</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="cloud-native-modernization">Cloud-native modernization</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Microsoft’s conviction from the start has been that modernization should become one of the fastest, most trusted, and most predictable workflows organizations undertake, and agentic AI is what finally makes that possible. For years, modernization and innovation competed for the same budget, and modernization usually lost. Now the math has changed: modernization is no longer the tax teams pay before they can innovate, it is the fastest path to it, and the gateway to AI. Eliminating decades of technical debt is what lets teams embed intelligence directly into the proven, high-value systems that already run their business. This modern foundation turns the assets they depend on most into the platform for what comes next.</p>



<div class="wp-block-buttons is-content-justification-center is-layout-flex wp-container-core-buttons-is-layout-a89b3969 wp-block-buttons-is-layout-flex">
<div class="wp-block-button"><a class="wp-block-button__link wp-element-button" href="https://www.gartner.com/reprints/?id=00ThR000008k1rGUAQ&amp;ct=260804&amp;st=sb" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Read the report</a></div>
</div>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">None of this is on the horizon; it is happening now. Modernization is shifting from a multi-year program into something teams finish in days or weeks.</p>


<figure data-wp-context="{&quot;imageId&quot;:&quot;6a9235a37a584&quot;}" data-wp-interactive="core/image" data-wp-key="6a9235a37a584" class="wp-block-image aligncenter size-large wp-lightbox-container"><img decoding="async" data-wp-class--hide="state.isContentHidden" data-wp-class--show="state.isContentVisible" data-wp-init="callbacks.setButtonStyles" data-wp-on--click="actions.showLightbox" data-wp-on--load="callbacks.setButtonStyles" data-wp-on-window--resize="callbacks.setButtonStyles" src="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/MQ-AppMod-Blog-Thumbnail-1-1-1024x576.webp" alt="Gartner graph, including completeness of vision and ability to execute." class="wp-image-53126 webp-format" srcset="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/MQ-AppMod-Blog-Thumbnail-1-1-1024x576.webp 1024w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/MQ-AppMod-Blog-Thumbnail-1-1-300x169.webp 300w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/MQ-AppMod-Blog-Thumbnail-1-1-768x432.webp 768w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/MQ-AppMod-Blog-Thumbnail-1-1-1536x864.webp 1536w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/MQ-AppMod-Blog-Thumbnail-1-1-809x455.webp 809w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/MQ-AppMod-Blog-Thumbnail-1-1.webp 1920w" data-orig-src="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/MQ-AppMod-Blog-Thumbnail-1-1-1024x576.webp"><button

<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Today, organizations are not just moving to the cloud or reducing technical debt, they are fundamentally rethinking their applications, their data, and their infrastructure because they know the decisions they make today will determine whether they are AI-ready tomorrow. Every modernization decision is now an AI decision.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Microsoft Azure was built for this moment. It provides the agentic foundation and engine for modernization and AI transformation, along with the governance and trust enterprises require, so modernization lowers security, data, and operational risk as much as it accelerates AI readiness. With agentic tooling and an end-to-end modernization solution powered by <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/solutions/application-and-database-modernization/#Products-3" id="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-in/products/github/copilot" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">GitHub Copilot</a> and <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/copilot" id="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/copilot" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure Copilot</a>, Microsoft is accelerating the shift, not just leading it.</p>


<figure data-wp-context="{&quot;imageId&quot;:&quot;6a9235a37c188&quot;}" data-wp-interactive="core/image" data-wp-key="6a9235a37c188" class="wp-block-image aligncenter size-large wp-lightbox-container"><img decoding="async" data-wp-class--hide="state.isContentHidden" data-wp-class--show="state.isContentVisible" data-wp-init="callbacks.setButtonStyles" data-wp-on--click="actions.showLightbox" data-wp-on--load="callbacks.setButtonStyles" data-wp-on-window--resize="callbacks.setButtonStyles" src="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/MQ_AgenticModDiagram-1-1024x575.webp" alt="Chart visualization of Agentic Modernization on Azure." class="wp-image-53127 webp-format" srcset="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/MQ_AgenticModDiagram-1-1024x575.webp 1024w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/MQ_AgenticModDiagram-1-300x169.webp 300w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/MQ_AgenticModDiagram-1-768x432.webp 768w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/MQ_AgenticModDiagram-1-1536x863.webp 1536w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/MQ_AgenticModDiagram-1-809x455.webp 809w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/MQ_AgenticModDiagram-1.webp 1920w" data-orig-src="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/MQ_AgenticModDiagram-1-1024x575.webp"><button

<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="the-problem-we-set-out-to-solve">The problem we set out to solve</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Every enterprise carries code it cannot afford to rewrite and cannot afford to ignore. Legacy .NET Framework applications, Java runtimes, aging dependencies, and accumulated security debt power critical business processes while also limiting an organization’s ability to move forward. For decades, modernization meant large teams, months of manual effort, and a real risk of breaking what already works. As a result, it got deferred, repeatedly, while technical debt compounded and the distance to the cloud grew.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">We feel Gartner frames the opportunity perfectly: software engineering leaders can meaningfully reduce the time, effort, and risk of modernization by using AI-powered tools rather than manual approaches. That distance, between the cost of standing still and the cost of moving, is exactly where we put our focus. The goal was never to help teams modernize a little faster. It was to fundamentally change the economics of modernization.</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="how-it-works">How it works</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The engine behind this is <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/solutions/application-and-database-modernization/#Products-3" id="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-in/products/github/copilot" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">GitHub Copilot</a> modernization, an agentic, end-to-end approach that helps teams assess, upgrade, and migrate applications to Azure, with AI handling repetitive tasks while developers remain firmly in control.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">We deliver it through two layers that work together. The modernization agent, available through the Modernize CLI, enables architects and application owners to orchestrate assessment, planning, and framework-upgrade automation across many applications at once, then delivers robust application-specific plans to developers. Inside the IDE, developers use GitHub Copilot to execute those plans: upgrading runtimes and frameworks, migrating dependencies to Azure services, generating infrastructure-as-code, and deploying to production. Assessment, planning, transformation, validation, and deployment become one continuous, guided journey rather than a dozen disconnected steps.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">What we chose not to automate matters just as much, and that is human judgment. Every recommendation is transparent, every change is reviewable, and every step is validated against your own tests and pipelines. This is modernization at machine speed, with the accountability enterprises require.</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="where-we-believe-we-stand-apart">Where we believe we stand apart</h2>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Breadth: </strong>Github Copilot modernization spans the languages that actually run the enterprise—.NET, Java, C++, and Python—rather than a single stack, so platform teams can standardize on one approach.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Repeatable expertise: </strong>Predefined tasks capture best practices for common migration patterns, such as adopting managed identities, <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/key-vault" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure Key Vault</a>, Blob and File storage, or <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-in/security/business/identity-access/microsoft-entra-id" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Entra ID</a>. Then custom skills let teams turn their own proven code changes into reusable patterns, applying them consistently across projects and ensuring institutional knowledge stays within the organization.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Trust built in: </strong>Security is part of the workflow, not a phase bolted on at the end. Copilot validates builds, migrates unit tests, scans for CVEs after upgrades, and applies fixes in agent mode—strengthening your security posture as you modernize, not after.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>Enterprise readiness</strong>: Modernizing one application is a project; modernizing an entire estate is a business imperative. What makes the difference at scale is consistency: the patterns that work become reusable tasks and skills any team can apply again, so a proven approach spreads across the portfolio instead of being reinvented app by app. It is what turns modernization from a string of one-off wins into a repeatable, governable practice.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item"><strong>The destination, not just the journey: </strong>Modernization is continuous. Applications land on Azure—<a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/app-service" id="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/app-service" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure App Service</a>, <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/container-apps" id="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/container-apps" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure Container Apps</a>, <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/kubernetes-service" id="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/kubernetes-service" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure Kubernetes Service</a>—and become ready to tap into the full system from silicon to software. This includes <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/ai-foundry" id="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/ai-foundry" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Foundry</a>’s catalog of more than 11,000 models and <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/ai/microsoft-iq" id="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/ai/microsoft-iq" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft IQ</a>, the enterprise intelligence layer that grounds AI agents in your organization&#8217;s own data and knowledge. Teams don’t just retire technical debt; they unlock what’s next.</li>
</ul>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="the-results-that-make-it-real">The results that make it real</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The numbers coming back from teams already doing this work are what change the conversation. Customers using GitHub Copilot modernization capabilities <a href="https://github.com/solutions/use-case/app-modernization" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">report up to 70% less time spent on migration efforts and 50% less effort to upgrade applications</a>. Some have moved more than 500,000 lines of code in a matter of weeks, work that once would have consumed quarters. Adoption tracks the same story: <a href="https://marketplace.visualstudio.com/items?itemName=vscjava.migrate-java-to-azure" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">more than 1.4 million developers have already installed the experience</a>.</p>



<blockquote class="wp-block-quote is-style-default has-quote-default-font-size is-layout-flow wp-block-quote-is-layout-flow">
<p class="wp-block-paragraph">GitHub Copilot’s modernization agent significantly saved time on our Java modernization effort from days down to mere hours at SAP Labs. It runs seamlessly at scale, significantly reducing manual complications for our developers. The modernization agent is now an essential pillar of our modernization effort.</p>
<cite>—Dr Richard Cai, Managing Director, SAP Labs China</cite></blockquote>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="this-is-the-starting-line">This is the starting line</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Being named a Leader in this Magic Quadrant is an honor for us, and candidly it is an obligation. The teams that adopt AI-powered modernization now will spend the next decade building on modern, secure foundations, in the cloud or hybrid, while others are still untangling the past. We intend to keep widening that gap with deeper language and framework coverage, richer agentic automation, and an ever-shorter path from legacy code to production on Azure. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">To every customer and partner who has modernized alongside us, thank you. You shaped this, and you are the reason for the recognition. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">If you are ready to see what is possible, explore agentic modernization and start with a single application this week. You can also <a href="https://www.gartner.com/reprints/?id=00ThR000008k1rGUAQ&amp;ct=260804&amp;st=sb" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">read a complimentary copy of the report to see why Microsoft was named a Leader</a>.</p>
